  • MVA-SARS-2-S 显示出良好的安全性,没有报告严重或严重的不良事件。然而,免疫原性较差,107 IU 和 108 IU 组中分别有 0/15 和 3/15 的参与者发生 S1 特异性血清转化。
  • 本研究共选取了 117 名参与者的 156 份血清样本。严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 IgG 浓度通常随着年龄的增长而增加,并在成年早期趋于稳定,但由于样本量较小,在较年轻的年龄组中的估计值并不精确。

  • XBB.1.5 vaccination was not associated with measurable protection against symptomatic infection (4%, 95% CI, -88 to 51). [10.1016/j.vaccine.2026.129242].

📘 主报告：研究论文 / Primary Research

Journal: Human Vaccines & Immunotherapeutics  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-10-15  |  DOI: 10.1080/21645515.2026.2728535  |  Date basis: online_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 22, 1, 2728535
A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

第一代改良痘苗病毒安卡拉载体 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗 候选物与基于 mRNA 的加强剂 疫苗接种 的安全性和免疫原性:1 期试验

Safety and immunogenicity of a first-generation modified vaccinia virus Ankara-vectored SARS-CoV-2 vaccine candidate with mRNA-based booster vaccination: A phase 1 trial
作者： Anahita Fathi, Christine Dahlke, Leonie Mayer, Sibylle C. Mellinghoff, Verena Krähling, Sandro Halwe, Helena Müller-Kräuter, Rolf Fendel, Peter G. Kremsner, Meral Esen
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在评估带有 mRNA 加强剂的第一代基于 MVA 的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗 候选药物的安全性和免疫原性,解决早期大流行期间对 疫苗 有效的 疫苗 的需求。
设计与对象
这项 1 期试验 (NCT04569383) 在 30 名健康、未接受过 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 治疗的成年人中评估了 MVA-SARS-2-S 疫苗 候选药物,这些成年人间隔 28 天接受两剂 107 或 108 IU 的 疫苗,随后进行 6 个月的随访。
核心方法
该研究以安全性和反应原性为主要目标,次要目标是测量 S1 血清转化率、S 结构域特异性抗体、严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 中和抗体和尖峰特异性 T 细胞反应。
主要结果
MVA-SARS-2-S 显示出良好的安全性,没有报告严重或严重的不良事件。然而,免疫原性较差,107 IU 和 108 IU 组中分别有 0/15 和 3/15 的参与者发生 S1 特异性血清转化。
解释与创新
作者得出的结论是,虽然 疫苗 具有良好的耐受性,但它引起的免疫原性不足,强调需要优化抗原设计以提高其有效性。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,为了使 MVA-SARS-2-S 疫苗 有效,需要进一步优化抗原设计,这可以为未来的 疫苗 开发策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究样本量小且随访有限,这可能会影响结果的普遍性。由于这些限制,证据的强度中等。

Safety and immunogenicity of a first-generation modified vaccinia virus Ankara-vectored SARS-CoV-2 vaccine candidate with mRNA-based booster vaccination: A phase 1 trial

Authors: Anahita Fathi, Christine Dahlke, Leonie Mayer, Sibylle C. Mellinghoff, Verena Krähling, Sandro Halwe, Helena Müller-Kräuter, Rolf Fendel, Peter G. Kremsner, Meral Esen
Original abstract (English)
The rapid development of effective COVID-19 vaccines was enabled by the parallel assessment of multiple candidates early in the pandemic, including candidates based on the Modified Vaccinia virus Ankara (MVA) vector platform. This Phase 1 trial (NCT04569383) evaluated the MVA-based vaccine candidate MVA-SARS-2-S, expressing the full-length native SARS-CoV-2 spike glycoprotein (Wuhan-HU-1). Between October 2020 and May 2021, 30 healthy, SARS-CoV-2–naive adults received two doses of either 107 or 108 international units (IU) of MVA-SARS-2-S 28 days apart, with 6-month follow-up. A subgroup (n = 12) received two BNT162b2 booster vaccinations. Safety and reactogenicity were primary objectives; secondary and exploratory objectives included S1 seroconversion rates, S-domain-specific antibodies, SARS-CoV-2-neutralizing antibodies, and spike-specific T cell responses. MVA-SARS-2-S demonstrated a good safety profile. No severe or serious adverse events occurred. Immunogenicity was poor: S1-specific seroconversion occurred in 0/15 (107 IU group) and 3/15 (108 IU group) participants. Binding antibodies were predominantly directed against the S2 subunit, with peak median anti-S2 fold changes of 6.0 and 5.2 in the 107 IU and 108 IU groups, respectively. Neutralizing antibodies were detected in one participant (108 IU group); spike-specific T cell responses were observed in 5/15 (107 IU group) and 2/13 (108 IU group) participants. Heterologous BNT162b2 vaccination induced S1 seroconversion and detectable neutralizing antibody and T cell responses in all boosted participants, without safety concerns. In conclusion, while MVA-SARS-2-S was well tolerated and did not adversely affect subsequent BNT162b2 boosting, the vaccine candidate elicited insufficient immunogenicity, underscoring the need for optimized antigen design.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to evaluate the safety and immunogenicity of a first-generation MVA-based SARS-CoV-2 vaccine candidate with an mRNA booster, addressing the need for effective vaccines against SARS-CoV-2 during the early pandemic.
Design and population
This Phase 1 trial (NCT04569383) assessed the MVA-SARS-2-S vaccine candidate in 30 healthy, SARS-CoV-2–naive adults who received two doses of either 107 or 108 IU of the vaccine 28 days apart, followed by a 6-month follow-up.
Core methods
The study focused on safety and reactogenicity as primary objectives, with secondary objectives measuring S1 seroconversion rates, S-domain-specific antibodies, SARS-CoV-2-neutralizing antibodies, and spike-specific T cell responses.
Main results
MVA-SARS-2-S showed a good safety profile, with no severe or serious adverse events reported. However, immunogenicity was poor, with S1-specific seroconversion occurring in 0/15 and 3/15 participants in the 107 IU and 108 IU groups, respectively.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors concluded that while the vaccine was well tolerated, it elicited insufficient immunogenicity, highlighting the need for optimized antigen design to improve its effectiveness.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that further optimization of the antigen design is necessary for the MVA-SARS-2-S vaccine to be effective, which could inform future vaccine development strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study had a small sample size and limited follow-up, which may affect the generalizability of the results. The evidence is moderate in strength due to these limitations.

纵向 BCR 库动态揭示了异源 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗接种 和突破性感染后广泛结合抗体的 进化:单一供体案例研究

Longitudinal BCR repertoire dynamics reveal the evolution of broadly binding antibodies following heterologous SARS-CoV-2 vaccination and breakthrough infection: a single-donor case study
作者： Rui Qiao, Xiaorong Chen, Xuhuan Zhang, Yuchen Shao, Liangjiu Zhang, Luxuan Yang, Yabo Mi, Yinying Lu, Jiayan Li, Yumeng Chen
摘要中文翻译持续的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 抗原漂移和人畜共患 sarbecovirus 跨物种溢出 的持续风险突出表明需要采取广泛的保护性对策。在这里,我们纵向分析了接受异源加强 疫苗接种 后接受 Omicron BF.7 突破性感染的单个志愿者的 B 细胞受体 (BCR) 库,将批量 BCR 测序与单个 B 细胞培养和抗体结合分析相结合。突破性感染与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体和相关 sarbecovirus 的血清中和作用扩大有关,这与针对保守表位的抗体的回忆一致。库分析揭示了阶段特异性重塑,高亲和力结合抗体从 疫苗接种 后富含 IGHV1-46 的谱转变为感染后优先表达 IGHV3-30-3。分离的单克隆抗体对多种动物源性 sarbecovirus 刺突表现出纳摩尔至皮摩尔的结合亲和力,通常具有广泛的结合,但并非普遍如此,与 14-16 个氨基酸的 CDRH3 长度相关。系统发育和序列分析定义了 IGHV1-46 和 IGHV3-30-3 谱系的不同进化轨迹,突出了需要在更大的 队列研究 中进行验证的候选遗传特征。总之,这些发现描述了连续的异源抗原暴露如何重塑个体的体液库,并为未来的泛沙病毒抗体和 疫苗 研究提供假设。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
持续的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 抗原漂移和人畜共患 sarbecovirus 跨物种溢出 的持续风险突出表明需要采取广泛的保护性对策。
设计与对象
在这里,我们纵向分析了接受异源加强 疫苗接种 后接受 Omicron BF.7 突破性感染的单个志愿者的 B 细胞受体 (BCR) 库,将批量 BCR 测序与单个 B 细胞培养和抗体结合分析相结合。
核心方法
突破性感染与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体和相关 sarbecovirus 的血清中和作用扩大有关,这与针对保守表位的抗体的回忆一致。
主要结果
库分析揭示了阶段特异性重塑,高亲和力结合抗体从 疫苗接种 后富含 IGHV1-46 的谱转变为感染后优先表达 IGHV3-30-3。
解释与创新
分离的单克隆抗体对多种动物源性 sarbecovirus 刺突表现出纳摩尔至皮摩尔的结合亲和力,通常具有广泛的结合,但并非普遍如此,与 14-16 个氨基酸的 CDRH3 长度相关。
科研与公卫意义
系统发育和序列分析定义了 IGHV1-46 和 IGHV3-30-3 谱系的不同进化轨迹,突出了需要在更大的 队列研究 中进行验证的候选遗传特征。
局限与证据强度
总之,这些发现描述了连续的异源抗原暴露如何重塑个体的体液库,并为未来的泛沙病毒抗体和 疫苗 研究提供假设。

Longitudinal BCR repertoire dynamics reveal the evolution of broadly binding antibodies following heterologous SARS-CoV-2 vaccination and breakthrough infection: a single-donor case study

Authors: Rui Qiao, Xiaorong Chen, Xuhuan Zhang, Yuchen Shao, Liangjiu Zhang, Luxuan Yang, Yabo Mi, Yinying Lu, Jiayan Li, Yumeng Chen
Original abstract (English)
Continued SARS-CoV-2 antigenic drift and the persistent risk of zoonotic sarbecovirus spillover highlight the need for broadly protective countermeasures. Here, we longitudinally profiled the B-cell receptor (BCR) repertoire of a single volunteer who received heterologous booster vaccination followed by Omicron BF.7 breakthrough infection, integrating bulk BCR sequencing with single B-cell culture and antibody binding analysis. Breakthrough infection was associated with broadened serum neutralization across SARS-CoV-2 variants and related sarbecoviruses, consistent with recall of antibodies targeting conserved epitopes. Repertoire analysis revealed stage-specific remodeling, with high-affinity binding antibodies shifting from an IGHV1-46-enriched profile after vaccination to preferential representation of IGHV3-30-3 after infection. Isolated monoclonal antibodies exhibited nanomolar-to-picomolar binding affinities to diverse animal-derived sarbecovirus spikes, with broad binding commonly, but not universally, associated with CDRH3 lengths of 14-16 amino acids. Phylogenetic and sequence analyses defined divergent evolutionary trajectories for IGHV1-46 and IGHV3-30-3 lineages, highlighting candidate genetic features that warrant validation in larger cohorts. Together, these findings describe how sequential heterologous antigen exposures can reshape the humoral repertoire in one individual and generate hypotheses for future pan-sarbecovirus antibody and vaccine studies.
View research seven-element analysis

Continued SARS-CoV-2 antigenic drift and the persistent risk of zoonotic sarbecovirus spillover highlight the need for broadly protective countermeasures.
Design and population
Here, we longitudinally profiled the B-cell receptor (BCR) repertoire of a single volunteer who received heterologous booster vaccination followed by Omicron BF.7 breakthrough infection, integrating bulk BCR sequencing with single B-cell culture and antibody binding analysis.
Core methods

Main results

A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

对 Omicron XBB.1.5 疫苗、既往感染及其在 Omicron KP.3 波期间针对症状性 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染诱导的中和抗体的保护

Protection of Omicron XBB.1.5 vaccine, previous infection, and their induced neutralizing antibodies against symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection during Omicron KP.3 wave
作者： Shohei Yamamoto, Kouki Matsuda, Kenji Maeda, Tetsuya Mizoue, Kumi Horii, Yusuke Oshiro, Natsumi Inamura, Takashi Nemoto, Yuichi Akahori, Junko S. Takeuchi
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
关于 Omicron XBB.1.5 疫苗、先前感染及其诱导的针对 Omicron KP.3 感染的中和抗体的保护作用的数据有限。
设计与对象
Among 1826 tertiary hospital staff in Tokyo who had received three or more doses of COVID-19 mRNA vaccines and provided blood samples in June 2024, we prospectively examined the associations of XBB.1.5 vaccination and previous infection with the risk of symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection during the Omicron KP.3 wave (June to September 2024).
核心方法
在巢式病例对照分析中,我们比较了病例和 1:1 倾向评分匹配对照之间的感染前活病毒 KP.3.3 中和抗体滴度。
主要结果
XBB.1.5 vaccination was not associated with measurable protection against symptomatic infection (4%, 95% CI, -88 to 51). Previous infection conferred protection that increased with its recency: 26% (95% CI, -26 to 56) for pre-Omicron XBB, 44% (95% CI, 2 to 68) for Omicron XBB, and 89% (95% CI, 52 to 97) for Omicron JN.1 waves.
解释与创新
以前的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染,特别是最近的 JN.1 波感染,提供了大量的中和滴度和保护。
科研与公卫意义
Pre-infection KP.3.3-neutralizing titers tended to be lower in cases than in matched controls (GMT ratio 0.68, 95% CI, 0.35 to 1.33); KP.3.3-neutralizing activity was only modestly elevated by XBB.1.5 vaccination but markedly higher after recent JN.1 infection.
局限与证据强度
在 Omicron KP.3 波期间,当更新的 疫苗 不可用时,大约 7 个月前施用的 XBB.1.5 疫苗接种 没有赋予可测量的中和活性或针对症状性 KP.3 感染的保护作用。

Protection of Omicron XBB.1.5 vaccine, previous infection, and their induced neutralizing antibodies against symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection during Omicron KP.3 wave

Authors: Shohei Yamamoto, Kouki Matsuda, Kenji Maeda, Tetsuya Mizoue, Kumi Horii, Yusuke Oshiro, Natsumi Inamura, Takashi Nemoto, Yuichi Akahori, Junko S. Takeuchi
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Data are limited on the protection of Omicron XBB.1.5 vaccine, previous infection, and their induced neutralizing antibodies against Omicron KP.3 infection. METHODS: Among 1826 tertiary hospital staff in Tokyo who had received three or more doses of COVID-19 mRNA vaccines and provided blood samples in June 2024, we prospectively examined the associations of XBB.1.5 vaccination and previous infection (classified by the timing of the last infection as pre-Omicron XBB, Omicron XBB, or Omicron JN.1 waves) with the risk of symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection during the Omicron KP.3 wave (June to September 2024). In a nested case-control analysis, we compared pre-infection live-virus KP.3.3-neutralizing antibody titers between cases and 1: 1 propensity-score matched controls. RESULTS: XBB.1.5 vaccination was not associated with measurable protection against symptomatic infection (4%, 95% CI, -88 to 51). Previous infection conferred protection that increased with its recency: 26% (95% CI, -26 to 56) for pre-Omicron XBB, 44% (95% CI, 2 to 68) for Omicron XBB, and 89% (95% CI, 52 to 97) for Omicron JN.1 waves. Pre-infection KP.3.3-neutralizing titers tended to be lower in cases than in matched controls (GMT ratio 0.68, 95% CI, 0.35 to 1.33); KP.3.3-neutralizing activity was only modestly elevated by XBB.1.5 vaccination but markedly higher after recent JN.1 infection. CONCLUSIONS: During the Omicron KP.3 wave, when an updated vaccine was unavailable, XBB.1.5 vaccination administered approximately 7 months earlier conferred no measurable neutralizing activity or protection against symptomatic KP.3 infection. Previous SARS-CoV-2 infection, particularly recent JN.1-wave infection, affords substantial neutralizing titers and protection.
View research seven-element analysis

Data are limited on the protection of Omicron XBB.1.5 vaccine, previous infection, and their induced neutralizing antibodies against Omicron KP.3 infection.
Design and population

Pre-infection KP.3.3-neutralizing titers tended to be lower in cases than in matched controls (GMT ratio 0.68, 95% CI, 0.35 to 1.33); KP.3.3-neutralizing activity was only modestly elevated by XBB.1.5 vaccination but markedly higher after recent JN.1 infection.

家庭流感中 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的前 Omicron 群体免疫力评估 疫苗 评估 (HIVE) 队列研究

Evaluation of pre-Omicron population immunity for SARS-CoV-2 in the Household Influenza Vaccine Evaluation (HIVE) cohort
作者： Yangyupei Yang, Casey L. Juntila-Raymond, Amy P. Callear, Matthew Smith, Reilly K. Atkinson, Yan Zhao, Ruhi Shah, Jefferson J. S. Santos, Scott E. Hensley, Arnold S. Monto
摘要中文翻译背景:持续进行 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 群体免疫血清调查对于 疫苗 菌株选择和易感群体(例如幼儿)的鉴定至关重要。我们的目标是评估使用高通量、低容量测定来描述基于 队列研究 的纵向跟踪社区中的菌株特异性 IgG 测量。方法:纵向家庭流感 疫苗 评估研究每年观察约 300 个家庭的急性呼吸道疾病。在 2021 年 7 月 1 日至 12 月 31 日期间抽取血清样本的参与者中,使用多重电化学发光 (ECL)、伪病毒中和 (PN) 和 ACE2 抑制测定法测量 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体特异性 IgG(包括原始、Delta、Beta 和 Omicron 变体)浓度。Spearman 等级相关系数评估了 ECL、ACE2 和 PN 测定内部和之间的相关性。受试者工作特征 (ROC) 分析评估了 ECL 和 ACE2 检测的性能。广义相加混合模型按年龄估计群体对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的免疫力。结果:本研究共选取了 117 名参与者的 156 份血清样本。大多数 (65.8%) 来自成人,大多数 (77.8%) 在分析期间接受了 疫苗接种。在 ECL IgG 检测 (0.95-1.00)、ACE2 抑制检测 (0.70-0.99) 以及检测平台之间观察到高度相关性,包括 ECL 与 PN (0.81-0.83) 以及 ACE2 与 PN (0.88-0.95)。严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 IgG 浓度通常随着年龄的增长而增加,并在成年早期趋于稳定,但由于样本量较小,在较年轻的年龄组中的估计值并不精确。结论:我们发现不同变体的 ECL、ACE2 和 PN 之间存在高度相关性。年龄特异性 IgG 数量反映了活跃的 监测 收集的行为和感染史。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
持续进行 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 群体免疫血清调查对于 疫苗 菌株选择和易感群体(例如幼儿)的鉴定至关重要。
设计与对象
我们的目标是评估使用高通量、低容量测定来描述基于 队列研究 的纵向跟踪社区中的菌株特异性 IgG 测量。
核心方法
纵向家庭流感 疫苗 评估研究每年观察约 300 个家庭是否患有急性呼吸道疾病。使用多重电化学发光 (ECL)、伪病毒中和 (PN) 和 ACE2 抑制测定对 2021 年 7 月 1 日至 12 月 31 日期间抽取血清样本的参与者测量 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体特异性 IgG(包括原始、Delta、Beta 和 Omicron 变体)浓度。在 ECL IgG 测定 (0.95-1.00) 和 ACE2 抑制测定中观察到高度相关性(0.70-0.99),以及检测平台之间的比较,包括 ECL 与 PN (0.81-0.83) 以及 ACE2 与 PN (0.88-0.95)。
主要结果
本研究共选取了 117 名参与者的 156 份血清样本。严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 IgG 浓度通常随着年龄的增长而增加,并在成年早期趋于稳定,但由于样本量较小,在较年轻的年龄组中的估计值并不精确。
解释与创新
我们发现不同变体的 ECL、ACE2 和 PN 之间存在高度相关性。年龄特异性 IgG 数量反映了活跃的 监测 收集的行为和感染史。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为疫苗毒株的选择以及识别易感群体(特别是年轻儿童)提供信息,从而了解人群对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株的免疫应答情况。
局限与证据强度
研究设计的局限性包括较年轻年龄组的样本量较小,这可能会影响年龄特异性 IgG 估计的精确度。证据强度中等。

Evaluation of pre-Omicron population immunity for SARS-CoV-2 in the Household Influenza Vaccine Evaluation (HIVE) cohort

Authors: Yangyupei Yang, Casey L. Juntila-Raymond, Amy P. Callear, Matthew Smith, Reilly K. Atkinson, Yan Zhao, Ruhi Shah, Jefferson J. S. Santos, Scott E. Hensley, Arnold S. Monto
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Continued serosurvey for SARS-CoV-2 population immunity is critical for vaccine strain selection and identification of susceptible groups such as young children. We aim to evaluate the use of a high-throughput, low-volume assay to describe strain-specific IgG measurement in a longitudinally followed community based cohort. METHODS: The longitudinal Household Influenza Vaccine Evaluation study has observed around 300 households annually for acute respiratory illness. SARS-CoV-2 variant-specific IgG (including original, Delta, Beta, and Omicron variants) concentrations were measured using multiplex electrochemiluminescence (ECL), pseudoviral neutralization (PN), and ACE2 inhibition assays in participants who had serum samples drawn between July 1 and December 31, 2021. Spearman's rank correlation coefficient assessed correlation within and between ECL, ACE2, and PN assays. Receiver operating characteristic (ROC) analysis evaluated the performance of ECL and ACE2 assays. Generalized additive mixed models estimated population immunity by age against SARS-CoV-2 variants. RESULTS: A total of 156 serum samples from 117 participants were selected for this study. Most (65.8%) were from adults and a majority (77.8%) received vaccination during the analysis period. High correlations were observed within ECL IgG assays (0.95-1.00), within ACE2 inhibition assays (0.70-0.99), and between assay platforms, including ECL versus PN (0.81-0.83) and ACE2 versus PN (0.88-0.95). SARS-CoV-2 IgG concentrations generally increased with age and appeared to plateau in early adulthood, although estimates were imprecise in younger age groups because of small sample sizes. CONCLUSION: We found a high correlation between ECL, ACE2, and PN across variants. Age-specific IgG quantities reflected behavior and infection histories collected by active surveillance.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Continued serosurvey for SARS-CoV-2 population immunity is critical for vaccine strain selection and identification of susceptible groups such as young children.
Design and population
We aim to evaluate the use of a high-throughput, low-volume assay to describe strain-specific IgG measurement in a longitudinally followed community based cohort.
Core methods
The longitudinal Household Influenza Vaccine Evaluation study has observed around 300 households annually for acute respiratory illness. SARS-CoV-2 variant-specific IgG (including original, Delta, Beta, and Omicron variants) concentrations were measured using multiplex electrochemiluminescence (ECL), pseudoviral neutralization (PN), and ACE2 inhibition assays in participants who had serum samples drawn between July 1 and December 31, 2021. High correlations were observed within ECL IgG assays (0.95-1.00), within ACE2 inhibition assays (0.70-0.99), and between assay platforms, including ECL versus PN (0.81-0.83) and ACE2 versus PN (0.88-0.95).
Main results
A total of 156 serum samples from 117 participants were selected for this study. SARS-CoV-2 IgG concentrations generally increased with age and appeared to plateau in early adulthood, although estimates were imprecise in younger age groups because of small sample sizes.
Interpretation and novelty
We found a high correlation between ECL, ACE2, and PN across variants. Age-specific IgG quantities reflected behavior and infection histories collected by active surveillance.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform vaccine strain selection and the identification of susceptible groups, particularly young children, by providing insights into population immunity against SARS-CoV-2 variants.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study design limitations include the small sample sizes in younger age groups, which may affect the precision of age-specific IgG estimates. The evidence strength is moderate.

Genomic surveillance of COVID-19 in Hong Kong (2020-2023): Analysing SARS-CoV-2 variants, mutations, and their impact on viral entry.

Genomic surveillance of COVID-19 in Hong Kong (2020-2023): Analysing SARS-CoV-2 variants, mutations, and their impact on viral entry.
作者： John David Pilapil, May Anne E. Mata, Ricardo C. H. del Rosario, Mark Joseph Pasciolco, King Lau Chow, King Lun Yeung
摘要中文翻译摘要:我们分析了 2020 年至 2023 年间从 NCBI 和 GISAID 获得的来自香港的 15,982 个质量控制的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 基因组 序列,以表征谱系动态、突变谱、人口统计分布和变体特异性病毒进入。基因组监测 鉴定了 543 个谱系和 5,982 个独特的 突变。A、B 和 B.4 谱系在最初的流行浪潮中占主导地位,而 Delta 和 Omicron 在后来的流行浪潮中出现并占主导地位。Omicron BA.1 和 BA.2 在性别和年龄组中表现出不同的分布,而 JN.1 则包含几个新颖的 突变。比较刺突受体结合域 (SRBD)-ACE2 相互作用的分子模拟揭示了变体之间结合亲和力的显着差异 (F = 4.404,p = 9.12 × 10−7)。JN.1 表现出增强的 ACE2 结合亲和力,表明病毒进入和潜在传播能力增加。通过整合基因组、人口统计和分子证据,本研究对香港的 进化 进化 进行了全面的描述,并强调了新出现的变异与公共卫生的相关性。这些发现支持持续的 基因组监测 和人口监测,以加强大流行病的防范。未来的研究应该评估公共卫生策略,并采用将人口特征与基因组证据相结合的整体框架,以减轻未来变异的影响。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在描述大流行期间香港 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的谱系动态、突变谱、人口分布和变体特异性病毒进入。
设计与对象
该研究分析了 2020 年至 2023 年间从 NCBI 和 GISAID 获得的 15,982 个质量控制的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 基因组 序列,涵盖了香港大流行的整个期间。
核心方法
研究人员使用分子模拟进行了 基因组监测,以比较变体之间的尖峰受体结合域 (SRBD)-ACE2 相互作用,揭示了结合亲和力的显着差异。
主要结果
该研究鉴定了 543 个谱系和 5,982 个独特的 突变,分子模拟显示变体之间的结合亲和力存在显着差异(F = 4.404,p = 9.12 × 10−7)。
解释与创新
Omicron BA.1 和 BA.2 在性别和年龄组中表现出不同的分布,而 JN.1 则包含几个新的 突变,表明病毒行为的潜在变化。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果强调了 基因组监测 和人口统计监测的重要性,以更好地理解和为新的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的出现做好准备。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其对公开基因组数据的依赖而受到限制,但多种证据类型的整合为其结论提供了中等程度的可信度。

Genomic surveillance of COVID-19 in Hong Kong (2020-2023): Analysing SARS-CoV-2 variants, mutations, and their impact on viral entry.

Authors: John David Pilapil, May Anne E. Mata, Ricardo C. H. del Rosario, Mark Joseph Pasciolco, King Lau Chow, King Lun Yeung
Original abstract (English)
Abstract We analysed 15,982 quality-controlled SARS-CoV-2 genome sequences from Hong Kong, obtained from NCBI and GISAID between 2020 and 2023, to characterize lineage dynamics, mutational profiles, demographic distributions, and variant-specific viral entry. Genomic surveillance identified 543 lineages and 5,982 unique mutations. Lineages A, B, and B.4 dominated the initial epidemic waves, whereas Delta and Omicron emerged and predominated in later waves. Omicron BA.1 and BA.2 exhibited distinct distributions across gender and age groups, while JN.1 harboured several novel mutations. Molecular simulations comparing spike receptor-binding domain (SRBD)–ACE2 interactions revealed significant differences in binding affinity among variants (F = 4.404, p = 9.12 × 10−7). JN.1 demonstrated enhanced ACE2-binding affinity, suggesting increased viral entry and potential transmissibility. By integrating genomic, demographic, and molecular evidence, this study provides a comprehensive account of SARS-CoV-2 evolution in Hong Kong and highlights the public health relevance of emerging variants. These findings support sustained genomic surveillance and demographic monitoring to strengthen pandemic preparedness. Future research should evaluate public health strategies and adopt holistic frameworks integrating population characteristics with genomic evidence to mitigate the effects of future variants.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to characterize the lineage dynamics, mutational profiles, demographic distributions, and variant-specific viral entry of SARS-CoV-2 in Hong Kong during the pandemic period.
Design and population
The study analyzed 15,982 quality-controlled SARS-CoV-2 genome sequences obtained from NCBI and GISAID between 2020 and 2023, covering the entire duration of the pandemic in Hong Kong.
Core methods
The researchers conducted genomic surveillance using molecular simulations to compare spike receptor-binding domain (SRBD)-ACE2 interactions among variants, revealing significant differences in binding affinity.
Main results
The study identified 543 lineages and 5,982 unique mutations, with molecular simulations showing significant differences in binding affinity among variants (F = 4.404, p = 9.12 × 10−7).
Interpretation and novelty
Omicron BA.1 and BA.2 exhibited distinct distributions across gender and age groups, while JN.1 harboured several novel mutations, indicating potential changes in viral behavior.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings highlight the importance of genomic surveillance and demographic monitoring to better understand and prepare for the emergence of new SARS-CoV-2 variants.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on publicly available genomic data, but the integration of multiple evidence types provides a moderate level of confidence in its conclusions.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 核衣壳蛋白通过靶向 eEF1A1 劫持 宿主 转录

SARS-CoV-2 nucleocapsid protein hijacks host transcription by targeting eEF1A1
作者： Baptiste Bidon, Yu-Lin Lai, Tai-Ling Chao, Ga-Wa Chen, Yee-Shin Lee, Hsiang-Hung Huang, Jia-Syuan Chen, Alejandro Gonzalez Losada, Sui-Yuan Chang, Jean-Marc Egly
摘要中文翻译严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体表现出巨大的遗传和表型多样性,但其对 宿主 基因调控影响的分子机制需要进一步研究。我们对 宿主 对多种 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体(包括 Delta 和 Omicron)的反应进行了转录组分析。尽管存在差异,但所有变体都会在人肺上皮细胞中诱导保守的转录程序,其特征是激活干扰素调节因子(IRF)、即早期基因和下游细胞因子。我们确定病毒核衣壳 (N) 蛋白是这种转录重编程的中心驱动因素,因为单独的 N 表达部分地概括了 宿主 对感染的反应。从机制上讲,N 促进干扰素调节因子和 AP-1 转录因子、RNA 聚合酶 II 和延伸因子 eEF1A1 募集到促炎基因的启动子,揭示了 eEF1A1 以前未被识别的核作用。使用 eEF1A 相互作用物 plitidepsin 从遗传或药理学上破坏 N-eEF1A1 相互作用,消除转录复合物形成并逆转 宿主 基因失调,识别保守的 宿主 病毒调节轴和潜在的泛变体治疗靶点。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在研究 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体影响 宿主 基因调控的分子机制,因为这些变体表现出巨大的遗传和表型多样性。
设计与对象
该研究涉及对人肺上皮细胞中 宿主 对多种 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体(包括 Delta 和 Omicron)的反应进行转录组分析。
核心方法
该研究使用转录组分析来检查 宿主 对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的反应,并确定核衣壳 (N) 蛋白通过与 宿主 因子相互作用作为转录重编程的中心驱动因素。
主要结果
研究发现,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的 N 蛋白可在人肺上皮细胞中诱导保守的转录程序,其特征是激活干扰素调节因子 (IRF)、即早期基因和下游细胞因子。
解释与创新
这些发现揭示了 eEF1A1 在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 诱导的转录变化中先前未被识别的核作用,突出了保守的 宿主 病毒调控轴。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果提供了对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 发病机制 分子机制的深入了解,并提出了对抗该病毒变体的潜在治疗靶点。
局限与证据强度
该研究的证据基于转录组分析,不包括直接的功能验证,这限制了结论的强度。

SARS-CoV-2 nucleocapsid protein hijacks host transcription by targeting eEF1A1

Authors: Baptiste Bidon, Yu-Lin Lai, Tai-Ling Chao, Ga-Wa Chen, Yee-Shin Lee, Hsiang-Hung Huang, Jia-Syuan Chen, Alejandro Gonzalez Losada, Sui-Yuan Chang, Jean-Marc Egly
Original abstract (English)
SARS-CoV-2 variants display substantial genetic and phenotypic diversity, yet the molecular mechanisms underlying their effects on host gene regulation need further investigation. We performed a transcriptomic analysis of host responses to multiple SARS-CoV-2 variants, including Delta and Omicron. Despite their divergence, all variants induced a conserved transcriptional program in human lung epithelial cells characterized by activation of interferon regulatory factors (IRFs), immediate–early genes, and downstream cytokines. We identify the viral nucleocapsid (N) protein as a central driver of this transcriptional reprogramming, as N expression alone partially recapitulated the host response to infection. Mechanistically, N promotes recruitment of interferon regulatory factors and AP-1 transcription factors, RNA polymerase II, and the elongation factor eEF1A1 to promoters of pro-inflammatory genes, revealing a previously unrecognized nuclear role for eEF1A1. Disruption of the N-eEF1A1 interaction, genetically or pharmacologically using the eEF1A interactant plitidepsin, abolished transcriptional complex formation and reversed host gene dysregulation, identifying a conserved host–virus regulatory axis and potential pan-variant therapeutic target.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the molecular mechanisms by which SARS-CoV-2 variants affect host gene regulation, as these variants display substantial genetic and phenotypic diversity.
Design and population
The research involved a transcriptomic analysis of host responses to multiple SARS-CoV-2 variants, including Delta and Omicron, in human lung epithelial cells.
Core methods
The study used transcriptomic analysis to examine host responses to SARS-CoV-2 variants and identified the nucleocapsid (N) protein as a central driver of transcriptional reprogramming through its interaction with host factors.
Main results
The N protein of SARS-CoV-2 was found to induce a conserved transcriptional program in human lung epithelial cells, characterized by activation of interferon regulatory factors (IRFs), immediate–early genes, and downstream cytokines.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings reveal a previously unrecognized nuclear role for eEF1A1 in SARS-CoV-2-induced transcriptional changes, highlighting a conserved host–virus regulatory axis.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results provide insights into the molecular mechanisms of SARS-CoV-2 pathogenesis and suggest potential therapeutic targets for combating the virus across its variants.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's evidence is based on transcriptomic analysis and does not include direct functional validation, which limits the strength of the conclusions.

A Genome-Wide Association Study in the Chilean admixed population reveals four new loci for COVID-19 severity and susceptibility

A Genome-Wide Association Study in the Chilean admixed population reveals four new loci for COVID-19 severity and susceptibility
作者： Espinosa-Parrilla, Y., Fuentes-Guajardo, M., Laura Carvajal-Silva, Aguilar-Valdes, K., Iskra A. Signore, Arevalo TV, Zapata-Contreras, D., Gerardo Donoso, Zuniga-Pacheco, P., Hector Valenzuela-Jorquera
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to identify genetic risk loci associated with COVID-19 severity and susceptibility in an admixed Latin American population, addressing the need to understand inter-individual and inter-population variability in disease outcomes.
设计与对象
A genome-wide association study was conducted on 3,417 individuals from Chile, representing a diverse admixed population, to analyze variations in COVID-19 clinical manifestations.
核心方法
The study utilized a genome-wide association approach, analyzing a cohort of 3,417 individuals from northern to southern Chile, focusing on genetic variants associated with COVID-19 severity and hospitalization.
主要结果
The study replicated known associations at the 3p21.31 locus and found suggestive associations for a variant within the FOXP4 gene at 6p21.1 with COVID-19 hospitalization. Additionally, three new loci (7q21.11, 16p13.12, and 6p25.3) were identified, along with one locus (4p13) associated with SARS-CoV-2 positivity, a proxy for infection susceptibility.
解释与创新
确定的新位点可能代表智利和其他潜在拉丁美洲混合群体中特定人群的影响,凸显了研究代表性不足的人群的重要性。
科研与公卫意义
The findings suggest that genetic factors play a role in COVID-19 severity and susceptibility, which could inform future surveillance, diagnosis, and treatment strategies, particularly in admixed populations.
局限与证据强度
该研究的局限性包括可能对特定人群产生影响以及需要在其他人群中进行复制。由于该研究的观察性质,证据强度中等。

A Genome-Wide Association Study in the Chilean admixed population reveals four new loci for COVID-19 severity and susceptibility

Authors: Espinosa-Parrilla, Y., Fuentes-Guajardo, M., Laura Carvajal-Silva, Aguilar-Valdes, K., Iskra A. Signore, Arevalo TV, Zapata-Contreras, D., Gerardo Donoso, Zuniga-Pacheco, P., Hector Valenzuela-Jorquera
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background More than five years after the COVID-19 outbreak, the infection by the SARS-CoV-2 virus should still be considered as a global threat with highly heterogeneous clinical outcomes from asymptomatic to fatal depending on existing genetic and phenotypic inter-individual and inter-population variability. Our objective is to identify risk loci associated with COVID-19 in an admixed Latin American population. Methods We performed a genome-wide association study in the framework of the Chilean C19-GenoNet team, analyzing a cohort of 3,417 individuals recruited from the northernmost to the southernmost regions of the country, who presented variations in the severity of their COVID-19 clinical manifestations. Results Our investigation replicated the extensively reported association of several variants at the 3p21.31 locus with COVID-19 severity and found a suggestive association for a variant within the FOXP4 gene at 6p21.1 with COVID-19 hospitalization. Both associations appear to be shared with other populations. Importantly, our study identified three previously unreported loci associated with COVID-19 severity or hospitalization at 7q21.11, 16p13.12 and 6p25.3, and one additional locus at 4p13 associated with SARS-CoV-2 positivity, a proxy for infection susceptibility. Conclusions these new risk loci may represent population-specific effects in Chilean and, potentially, other Latin American admixed groups. Our study highlights the importance of expanding efforts to study underrepresented and genetically different populations, which may provide new clues on the genetic basis of human diseases and may help to anticipate global responses to emerging viral infections and future pandemics.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to identify genetic risk loci associated with COVID-19 severity and susceptibility in an admixed Latin American population, addressing the need to understand inter-individual and inter-population variability in disease outcomes.
Design and population
A genome-wide association study was conducted on 3,417 individuals from Chile, representing a diverse admixed population, to analyze variations in COVID-19 clinical manifestations.
Core methods
The study utilized a genome-wide association approach, analyzing a cohort of 3,417 individuals from northern to southern Chile, focusing on genetic variants associated with COVID-19 severity and hospitalization.
Main results
The study replicated known associations at the 3p21.31 locus and found suggestive associations for a variant within the FOXP4 gene at 6p21.1 with COVID-19 hospitalization. Additionally, three new loci (7q21.11, 16p13.12, and 6p25.3) were identified, along with one locus (4p13) associated with SARS-CoV-2 positivity, a proxy for infection susceptibility.
Interpretation and novelty
The identified new loci may represent population-specific effects in Chilean and potentially other Latin American admixed groups, highlighting the importance of studying underrepresented populations.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that genetic factors play a role in COVID-19 severity and susceptibility, which could inform future surveillance, diagnosis, and treatment strategies, particularly in admixed populations.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's limitations include the potential for population-specific effects and the need for replication in other populations. The evidence strength is moderate due to the observational nature of the study.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 二次暴露期间纵隔淋巴结生发中心动态区分时间依赖性和变异特异性免疫

Germinal center dynamics in the mediastinal lymph node distinguish time-dependent and variant-specific immunity during SARS-CoV-2 secondary exposure
作者： Alyson A. Kelvin, Opeyemi Ernest Oludada, Magen Francis, Baweleta Isho, Salma Sheikh-Mohamed, Albert Phan Nguyen, Reema Singh, Cynthia L. Swan, Joseph Darbellay, Angie Rassmussen
摘要中文翻译摘要:即使个体先前对自然感染或 疫苗接种 具有免疫力,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 继发感染仍然是一个持续的挑战。然而,对于决定二次暴露是否受到预先存在的免疫力快速控制并导致消毒保护或进展为生产性感染和疾病的细胞机制仍知之甚少。在 C57BL/6J 小鼠中使用同源和异源二次攻击实验策略,我们表明,对先前感染的小鼠的同源 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Alpha 二次攻击的保护是有时间限制的,在初次感染后 56 天保持持久,在初次感染后第 84 天突破感染。这种保护的丧失与中和抗体滴度的减弱无关,而是与纵隔淋巴结中滤泡辅助T细胞和生发中心B细胞的进行性收缩相关。相比之下,先前感染α的小鼠中的异源β二次暴露始终未能产生强有力的生发中心反应,尽管与同源再次攻击相比动员了相当或更多数量的免疫细胞。相反,RNA-seq 和流式细胞术分析表明,这种反应与滤泡外 B 细胞炎症反应相关,其特征是 Bcl6 和 Aicda 表达减少,同时 Il6 强烈上调。总而言之,这些发现确定纵隔淋巴结中的生发中心回忆反应是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 二次免疫的重要细胞相关性,并对加强免疫的时机和交叉变体 疫苗 的设计具有直接影响。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在了解决定二次 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 暴露是否会导致灭菌保护或生产性感染的细胞机制,尽管预先存在自然感染或 疫苗接种 的免疫力。
设计与对象
该研究使用 C57BL/6J 小鼠进行同源和异源二次攻击实验,以研究 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 免疫。
核心方法
该研究在 C57BL/6J 小鼠中采用同源和异源二次攻击实验策略,利用 RNA-seq 和流式细胞术分析来评估 免疫应答。
主要结果
针对同源 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Alpha 二次攻击的保护是有时间限制的,突破性感染发生在初次感染后第 84 天。先前感染 Alpha 的小鼠中,异源 Beta 二次暴露并未引起强烈的生发中心反应,而是表现出炎症性滤泡外 B 细胞反应。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,纵隔淋巴结中的生发中心回忆反应是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 二次免疫的关键相关性,对加强免疫时机和跨变体 疫苗 设计具有影响。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现对加强免疫的时机和交叉变体 疫苗 的设计有直接影响,强调了生发中心回忆反应在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 二次免疫中的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究的结果受到动物模型的使用和所使用的特定小鼠品系的限制,可能无法完全推广到人类群体。

Germinal center dynamics in the mediastinal lymph node distinguish time-dependent and variant-specific immunity during SARS-CoV-2 secondary exposure

Authors: Alyson A. Kelvin, Opeyemi Ernest Oludada, Magen Francis, Baweleta Isho, Salma Sheikh-Mohamed, Albert Phan Nguyen, Reema Singh, Cynthia L. Swan, Joseph Darbellay, Angie Rassmussen
Original abstract (English)
Abstract SARS-CoV-2 secondary infections remain a persistent challenge even though individuals have previous immunity from natural infection or vaccination. Yet, the cellular mechanisms that determine whether a secondary exposure is rapidly controlled by pre-existing immunity and results in sterilizing protection or progresses to productive infection and disease remain poorly understood. Using homologous and heterologous secondary-challenge experimental strategies in C57BL/6J mice, we show that protection against a homologous SARS-CoV-2 Alpha secondary challenge in previously infected mice is time-limited, remaining durable through 56 days post-primary infection, with breakthrough infection on day 84 post-primary infection. This loss of protection was not associated with waning neutralizing antibody titers but instead correlated with progressive contraction of T follicular helper cells and germinal center B cells in the mediastinal lymph node. In contrast, heterologous Beta secondary exposure in Alpha-previously infected mice consistently failed to mount a robust germinal center response despite mobilizing comparable or greater numbers of immune cells than homologous re-challenge. Instead, RNA-seq and flow cytometry analyses revealed that this response was associated with an inflammatory extrafollicular B cell response characterized by reduced Bcl6 and Aicda expression and concomitant robust upregulation of Il6 . Taken together, these findings identify the germinal center recall response in the mediastinal lymph node as an important cellular correlate of SARS-CoV-2 secondary immunity and carry direct implications for the timing of booster immunization and the design of cross-variant vaccines.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to understand the cellular mechanisms determining whether a secondary SARS-CoV-2 exposure results in sterilizing protection or productive infection, despite pre-existing immunity from natural infection or vaccination.
Design and population
The study used C57BL/6J mice for homologous and heterologous secondary-challenge experiments to investigate SARS-CoV-2 immunity.
Core methods
The study employed homologous and heterologous secondary-challenge experimental strategies in C57BL/6J mice, utilizing RNA-seq and flow cytometry analyses to assess immune responses.
Main results
Protection against a homologous SARS-CoV-2 Alpha secondary challenge was time-limited, with breakthrough infection occurring on day 84 post-primary infection. Heterologous Beta secondary exposure in Alpha-previously infected mice did not mount a robust germinal center response, instead showing an inflammatory extrafollicular B cell response.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that the germinal center recall response in the mediastinal lymph node is a key correlate of SARS-CoV-2 secondary immunity, with implications for booster immunization timing and cross-variant vaccine design.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings have direct implications for the timing of booster immunization and the design of cross-variant vaccines, highlighting the importance of the germinal center recall response in SARS-CoV-2 secondary immunity.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's findings are limited by the use of animal models and the specific strain of mice used, which may not fully generalize to human populations.

Vaccination and Non-School NPIs Shaped Ontario's Pre-Omicron COVID-19 Hospital Burden

Vaccination and Non-School NPIs Shaped Ontario's Pre-Omicron COVID-19 Hospital Burden
作者： Marwa Z. Tuffaha, David W. Dick, Jing Li, Jane Marie Heffernan, Tuffaha, M. Z., Dick, D. W., Heffernan, J. M.
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
This study aimed to evaluate how vaccination and non-school non-pharmaceutical interventions (NPIs) influenced the hospital burden of COVID-19 in Ontario before the Omicron variant emerged, addressing the need to understand the impact of these measures on severe disease outcomes.
设计与对象
该研究使用反事实模拟方法来模拟不同的 疫苗接种 策略以及工作场所和社区环境中的接触限制以及学校限制对前 Omicron 时期安大略省医院床位预计需求的影响。
核心方法
研究人员开发了一种根据安大略省的 发病率 和入院数据进行校准的年龄和免疫结构模型,模拟 疫苗接种 和接触限制的各种场景,以评估其对重症病例需求的影响。
主要结果
即使学校限制仍然存在,工作场所或社区环境中放宽接触限制也会导致重症病例需求大幅激增。仅学校的变化对严重疾病结果的影响较小。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,在成人为主的环境中,将 疫苗接种 与有针对性的接触限制相结合,比仅仅依靠基于学校的干预措施更能有效地管理医院需求。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果强调了优先考虑工作场所和社区接触限制以及 疫苗接种 的重要性,为公共卫生策略提供了信息,以减轻 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的严重后果。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖于模拟数据,没有考虑现实世界的变异性,这可能会限制其研究结果的普遍性。由于分析基于模型的性质,证据的强度中等。

Vaccination and Non-School NPIs Shaped Ontario's Pre-Omicron COVID-19 Hospital Burden

Authors: Marwa Z. Tuffaha, David W. Dick, Jing Li, Jane Marie Heffernan, Tuffaha, M. Z., Dick, D. W., Heffernan, J. M.
Original abstract (English)
We develop an age- and immunity-structured COVID-19 model with setting-specific contacts, calibrated to Ontario incidence and hospital admissions before Omicron. We simulate counterfactual vaccination strategies and contact restrictions in schools, workplaces, and other community settings, evaluating projected severe-case demand against an illustrative healthcare-capacity benchmark. Vaccination combined with workplace and community contact restrictions keeps simulated demand within capacity. In contrast, relaxing restrictions in either setting produces large surges and repeated capacity threats, even when school restrictions remain. School-only changes produce smaller effects on severe disease. These findings highlight the value of vaccination and setting-specific contact restrictions that prioritize workplace and community settings for pathogens with adult-concentrated severe outcomes.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to evaluate how vaccination and non-school non-pharmaceutical interventions (NPIs) influenced the hospital burden of COVID-19 in Ontario before the Omicron variant emerged, addressing the need to understand the impact of these measures on severe disease outcomes.
Design and population
The study used a counterfactual simulation approach to model the effects of different vaccination strategies and contact restrictions in workplaces and community settings, alongside school restrictions, on the projected demand for hospital beds in Ontario during the pre-Omicron period.
Core methods
The researchers developed an age- and immunity-structured model calibrated to Ontario's incidence and hospital admission data, simulating various scenarios of vaccination and contact restrictions to assess their impact on severe-case demand.
Main results
Relaxing contact restrictions in workplaces or community settings led to large surges in severe-case demand, even when school restrictions remained in place. School-only changes had smaller effects on severe disease outcomes.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings demonstrate that combining vaccination with targeted contact restrictions in adult-dominated settings is more effective in managing hospital demand than relying solely on school-based interventions.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results inform public health strategies by emphasizing the importance of prioritizing workplace and community contact restrictions alongside vaccination to mitigate severe outcomes of SARS-CoV-2.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relies on simulated data and does not account for real-world variability, which may limit the generalizability of its findings. The evidence is moderate in strength due to the model-based nature of the analysis.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 关注变体背景下肺癌的病理生理学

Pathophysiology of Lung Cancer in the Context of SARS-CoV-2 Variants of Concern
作者： Angela Mathews
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在解决有关 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变异背景下肺癌病理生理学的知识差距以及这些变异对患者结果的影响。
设计与对象
This retrospective cohort study examined the clinical manifestation of SARS-CoV-2 infection in patients with lung cancer, focusing on the molecular characteristics driving severe COVID-19 and the role of inflammation in accelerating lung cancer severity, particularly with respect to the Alpha, Beta, Gamma, and Delta variants.
核心方法
该研究利用回顾性 队列研究 数据来分析肺癌和 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变异之间的关系,重点是了解影响患者预后的分子和炎症机制。
主要结果
回顾性队列研究研究显示,癌症患者,包括肺癌患者,由于免疫抑制和合并症等因素,感染严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2后面临更高的严重并发症和死亡率风险。
解释与创新
作者认为,了解肺癌和 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 之间的串扰,特别是早期变异,将揭示导致不良结果的独特机制,例如高炎症状态和免疫逃避。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可能为癌症人群改进 抗病毒药物 和 疫苗 的开发提供信息,并为肺癌患者中当前和新出现的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的 发病机制 提供见解。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其回顾性而受到限制,并且需要进行额外的研究才能充分了解 Omicron 等新变体对肺癌患者的影响。

Pathophysiology of Lung Cancer in the Context of SARS-CoV-2 Variants of Concern

Authors: Angela Mathews
Original abstract (English)
Many retrospective cohort studies have revealed that patients with cancer are at increased risk of severe complications and mortality from COVID-19 due to a multitude of factors, including advanced age in this cohort, high incidence of underlying comorbidities, and immunosuppression caused by cancer and its treatments. It has been futher demonstrated that individuals with lung cancer face a disproportionately higher risk of mortality compared to other cancer types following SARS-SoV-2 infection. However, several questions remain unanswered regarding the understanding of the pathophysiology of lung cancer in the context of COVID-19, as well as the effects of SARS-CoV-2 variants of concern on patient outcomes. The article aims to investigate the following gaps the clinical manifestation of SARS-CoV-2 infection in patients with lung cancer: the molecular characteristics of lung cancer driving the development of severe COVID-19, and the role of COVID-19-induced inflammation in accelerating the development and increasing the severity of lung cancer, particularly with respect to the Alpha (B.1.1.7), Beta (B.1.351), Gamma (P.1) and Delta (B.1.617.2) variants. Understanding the crosstalk between lung cancer and COVID-19, particularly concerning the early variants of concern, will reveal the unique mechanisms contributing to poor outcomes following SARS-CoV-2 in patients with lung cancer, including the maintenance of a hyperinflammatory state, TMPRSS2 expression patterns, and tumor-associated immune evasion, among others. This work will be valuable in the development of improved antivirals and vaccines against SARS-CoV-2 in the cancer population and will help provide insight into the pathogenesis of current and emerging variants of concern in this at-risk cohort. Additional studies and a high degree of caution are essential in understanding the impacts of newer and emerging variants of concern such as Omicron (B.1.1.529) and its subvariants on patients with lung cancer.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the knowledge gap regarding the pathophysiology of lung cancer in the context of SARS-CoV-2 variants of concern and the effects of these variants on patient outcomes.
Design and population
This retrospective cohort study examined the clinical manifestation of SARS-CoV-2 infection in patients with lung cancer, focusing on the molecular characteristics driving severe COVID-19 and the role of inflammation in accelerating lung cancer severity, particularly with respect to the Alpha, Beta, Gamma, and Delta variants.
Core methods
The study utilized retrospective cohort data to analyze the relationship between lung cancer and SARS-CoV-2 variants, with an emphasis on understanding the molecular and inflammatory mechanisms influencing patient outcomes.
Main results
Retrospective cohort studies have shown that patients with cancer, including lung cancer, face increased risk of severe complications and mortality from SARS-CoV-2 infection due to factors such as immunosuppression and comorbidities.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors suggest that understanding the crosstalk between lung cancer and SARS-CoV-2, particularly with early variants, will uncover unique mechanisms contributing to poor outcomes, such as hyperinflammatory states and immune evasion.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings may inform the development of improved antivirals and vaccines for the cancer population and provide insights into the pathogenesis of current and emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants in patients with lung cancer.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its retrospective nature and the need for additional research to fully understand the impacts of newer variants like Omicron on patients with lung cancer.

通过 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的预测数学模型告知风险分层和 疫苗 政策

Informing risk stratification and vaccine policy via a predictive mathematical model of SARS-COV-2 infection
作者： Benny Chain, Curt Callan, David Scobie, Matthew Cowley, Jana Jang, Rishi K Gupta, Carolin T. Turner, Joshua Rosenheim, Clare E Thakker, Lucy C. K. Bell
摘要中文翻译摘要 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 人类挑战研究实现了病毒控制免疫决定因素前所未有的高频时间测量。我们将 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 复制、先天细胞毒性 T 细胞和抗体反应的先验知识整合到一个动态模型中,该模型捕获了接受野生型病毒攻击的血清阴性志愿者的病毒载量动力学。该模型预测了测量到的先天和适应性免疫动态。扰动分析表明,先天免疫限制了病毒载量峰值,而抗体主要介导清除,而细胞毒性 T 细胞在抗体缺乏中变得至关重要。预先存在的抗体抑制了病毒复制和随后的免疫激活——这一预测在感染 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Delta 变体的血清阳性参与者的第二项挑战研究中得到了验证。该模型解释了特异性免疫缺陷如何增加对严重或持续感染的易感性,支持临床风险分层。这表明,在没有有效中和抗体的情况下,针对 T 细胞的 疫苗接种 策略可能提供有限的保护,并且低剂量暴露后减弱的增强作用需要重复 疫苗接种 来减少突破性感染,从而为公共卫生政策提供信息。一句话总结 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的数学模型可以预测先天性和适应性免疫,并为风险分层和 疫苗 策略提供信息。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在通过了解 免疫应答 在控制 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染中的作用,为风险分层和 疫苗 政策提供信息。
设计与对象
使用接受野生型 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 攻击的血清阴性志愿者的数据开发了一个预测数学模型,捕获病毒载量动力学和免疫动力学。
核心方法
该模型整合了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 复制、先天、细胞毒性 T 细胞和抗体反应的先验知识,并通过扰动分析和血清阳性参与者的第二次挑战研究进行了验证。
主要结果
扰动分析表明,先天免疫限制了病毒载量峰值,而抗体主要介导清除,而细胞毒性 T 细胞在抗体缺乏中变得至关重要。预先存在的抗体抑制了病毒复制和免疫激活,这一发现在第二项挑战研究中得到了验证。
解释与创新
该模型提供了关于特定免疫缺陷如何增加对严重或持续感染的易感性的新见解,支持临床风险分层。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果支持临床风险分层工具的开发,并通过强调中和抗体在保护 against 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 中的重要性,为 疫苗 政策提供依据。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于数学模型和挑战研究,这可能对现实世界人群的普遍性有限,但证据得到了第二次挑战研究验证的支持。

Informing risk stratification and vaccine policy via a predictive mathematical model of SARS-COV-2 infection

Authors: Benny Chain, Curt Callan, David Scobie, Matthew Cowley, Jana Jang, Rishi K Gupta, Carolin T. Turner, Joshua Rosenheim, Clare E Thakker, Lucy C. K. Bell
Original abstract (English)
Abstract SARS-CoV-2 human challenge studies enabled unprecedented high frequency temporal measurements of immune determinants of viral control. We integrated prior knowledge of SARS-CoV-2 replication, innate, cytotoxic T cell and antibody responses into a dynamic model which captured viral-load kinetics in seronegative volunteers challenged with wildtype virus. The model predicted measured innate and adaptive immune dynamics. Perturbation analyses indicated that innate immunity limits peak viral load, whereas antibodies principally mediate clearance while cytotoxic T cells become critical in antibody deficiency. Pre-existing antibodies suppressed both viral replication and subsequent immune activation- a prediction validated in a second challenge study of seropositive participants infected with the SARS-CoV-2 Delta variant. The model explains how specific immunodeficiencies may increase susceptibility to severe or persistent infection, supporting clinical risk stratification. It suggests that T cell-targeted vaccination strategies may provide limited protection without effective neutralising antibodies, and that attenuated boosting after low-dose exposure requires repeated vaccination to reduce breakthrough infections, informing public health policy. One Sentence Summary A mathematical model of SARS-COV-2 infection predicts innate and adaptive immunity, and informs risk stratification and vaccine policy.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to inform risk stratification and vaccine policy by understanding the role of immune responses in controlling SARS-CoV-2 infection.
Design and population
A predictive mathematical model was developed using data from seronegative volunteers challenged with wildtype SARS-CoV-2, capturing viral-load kinetics and immune dynamics.
Core methods
The model integrated prior knowledge of SARS-CoV-2 replication, innate, cytotoxic T cell and antibody responses, and was validated through perturbation analyses and a second challenge study of seropositive participants.
Main results
Perturbation analyses indicated that innate immunity limits peak viral load, whereas antibodies primarily mediate clearance, and cytotoxic T cells become critical in antibody deficiency. Pre-existing antibodies suppressed both viral replication and immune activation, a finding validated in a second challenge study.
Interpretation and novelty
The model provides novel insights into how specific immunodeficiencies may increase susceptibility to severe or persistent infection, supporting clinical risk stratification.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings support the development of clinical risk stratification tools and inform vaccine policy by highlighting the importance of neutralising antibodies in protection against SARS-CoV-2.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on a mathematical model and challenge studies, which may have limited generalisability to real-world populations, but the evidence is supported by validation in a second challenge study.

Immune parameters in hospitalized COVID-19 patients during SARS-CoV2 delta and omicron waves.

Immune parameters in hospitalized COVID-19 patients during SARS-CoV2 delta and omicron waves.
作者： Geraldo S Magalhães, Sarah N C Gimenes, Bianca C. L. F. Távora, José A Portes Junior, Paula A L Calabria, Leonel I. Montealegre-Sanchez, Sandra C Sampaio Vessoni, Eduardo Massad, Celina A Lamas, Sergio S. Muller
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Understanding immune mechanisms associated with COVID-19 severity remains important for interpreting clinical outcomes.
设计与对象
We investigated virological and immunological profiles in 39-patients hospitalized with COVID-19 at InCor Hospital during the Delta and Omicron waves and included 28-non-hospitalized individuals with moderate COVID-19 as a comparator group for cytokine analyses.
核心方法
通过鼻咽拭子的 RT-PCR 证实了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染,并通过下一代测序鉴定了病毒变异体。RNAemia 通过 RT-PCR 进行评估并作为二元变量进行分析。通过细胞计数珠阵列对血清细胞因子进行定量,同时使用 CLIA 和基于 Wuuu-Hu-1 的假病毒中和测定通过抗 Spike RBD IgG 水平评估体液反应。
主要结果
Cytokine profiling showed increased IL-6, IL-8, TNF, IL-17 A and IL-10 levels in hospitalized patients compared with moderate COVID-19 individuals, but did not distinguish recovered from deceased patients. Neutralizing antibody titers measured against a Wuhan-Hu-1 spike pseudovirus were significantly higher in recovered individuals than decease patients, underscoring the relevance of antibody functionality.
解释与创新
These data emphasize the relevance of viral dissemination and functional humoral immunity as potential biomarkers for COVID-19 progression and outcome, with RNAemia and neutralizing antibody levels showing a stronger exploratory association in this cohort.
科研与公卫意义
RNAemia and neutralizing antibody levels may serve as biomarkers for COVID-19 progression and outcome, highlighting the importance of viral dissemination and functional humoral immunity.
局限与证据强度
研究设计的局限性包括样本量小和缺乏随机对照组,这可能会影响研究结果的普遍性。证据强度中等。

Immune parameters in hospitalized COVID-19 patients during SARS-CoV2 delta and omicron waves.

Authors: Geraldo S Magalhães, Sarah N C Gimenes, Bianca C. L. F. Távora, José A Portes Junior, Paula A L Calabria, Leonel I. Montealegre-Sanchez, Sandra C Sampaio Vessoni, Eduardo Massad, Celina A Lamas, Sergio S. Muller
Original abstract (English)
Understanding immune mechanisms associated with COVID-19 severity remains important for interpreting clinical outcomes. We investigated virological and immunological profiles in 39-patients hospitalized with COVID-19 at InCor Hospital during the Delta and Omicron waves and included 28-non-hospitalized individuals with moderate COVID-19 as a comparator group for cytokine analyses. SARS-CoV-2 infection was confirmed by RT-PCR from nasopharyngeal swabs, and viral variants were identified by next-generation sequencing. RNAemia was assessed by RT-PCR and analyzed as a binary variable. Serum cytokines were quantified by cytometric bead array, while humoral responses were evaluated by anti-Spike RBD IgG levels using CLIA and a Wuhan-Hu-1-based pseudovirus neutralization assay. RNAemia was more frequent in ICU-admitted patients than in non-ICU patients, supporting an association with disease severity. Cytokine profiling showed increased IL-6, IL-8, TNF, IL-17 A and IL-10 levels in hospitalized patients compared with moderate COVID-19 individuals, but did not distinguish recovered from deceased patients. Neutralizing antibody titers measured against a Wuhan-Hu-1 spike pseudovirus were significantly higher in recovered individuals than decease patients, underscoring the relevance of antibody functionality. These data emphasize the relevance of viral dissemination and functional humoral immunity as potential biomarkers for COVID-19 progression and outcome, with RNAemia and neutralizing antibody levels showing a stronger exploratory association in this cohort.

Scientific and public-health significance
RNAemia and neutralizing antibody levels may serve as biomarkers for COVID-19 progression and outcome, highlighting the importance of viral dissemination and functional humoral immunity.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study design limitations include the small sample size and the absence of a randomized control group, which may affect the generalizability of the findings. The evidence strength is moderate.

Impact of Community Factors on Mental and Physical Health During Successive Waves of COVID-19 Pandemic in China.

Impact of Community Factors on Mental and Physical Health During Successive Waves of COVID-19 Pandemic in China.
作者： Gigi Lam, Catherine So-Kum Tang, Tiffany Sok U Siu, Tak Sang Chow
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
This study examined how community factors impacted well-being during the COVID-19 pandemic in China.
设计与对象
Data were collected at two time points: Time 1 (T1) in September 2021 (N = 992) when COVID-19 cases stabilized, and Time 2 (T2) in January 2023 (N = 497) during a sudden outbreak of new infected cases.
核心方法
在T1,感知的社区安全和社区意识直接影响抑郁、焦虑和身体健康症状,而社区意识部分介导了这些影响。
主要结果
在 T2 时,T1 时的社区因素继续影响抑郁、焦虑和身体健康症状。
解释与创新
T1 的社区意识部分调节了 T1 的社区安全与 T2 的心理健康之间的联系。
科研与公卫意义
较高的收入和较高的年龄与更强的社区意识、更好的社区安全感和更好的健康状况相关。
局限与证据强度
政策制定者应考虑健康危机期间社区因素对福祉的影响。

Impact of Community Factors on Mental and Physical Health During Successive Waves of COVID-19 Pandemic in China.

Authors: Gigi Lam, Catherine So-Kum Tang, Tiffany Sok U Siu, Tak Sang Chow
Original abstract (English)
This study examined how community factors impacted well-being during the COVID-19 pandemic in China. Data were collected at two time points: Time 1 (T1) in September 2021 (N = 992) when COVID-19 cases stabilized, and Time 2 (T2) in January 2023 (N = 497) during a sudden outbreak of new infected cases. At T1, perceived community safety and sense of community directly affected depression, anxiety, and physical health symptoms, with sense of community partially mediating these effects. At T2, community factors at T1 continued to impact depression, anxiety, and physical health symptoms. Sense of community at T1 partially mediated the link between community safety at T1 and mental health at T2. Higher income and older age were associated with a stronger sense of community, better perceived community safety, and improved health. Policymakers should consider the impact of community factors on well-being during health crises.

Severity-dependent and sex-biased dysregulation of A-to-I RNA editing in COVID-19 reveals CTSS as a key regulatory target

Severity-dependent and sex-biased dysregulation of A-to-I RNA editing in COVID-19 reveals CTSS as a key regulatory target
作者： Jing-Yuan Wang, Zhao Ya-di, Li-Ping Wang, Yu-Qing Ren, Jia-Nuo Li, Teng Wang, Hua-Kang Zhang, Feng Zhu, Jian‐Huan Chen, Yun-Yun Jin
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
SARS-CoV-2 infection causes diverse clinical outcomes, with men facing higher risks of severe disease. Adenosine-to-inosine (A-to-I) RNA editing mediated by ADAR enzymes governs innate antiviral immunity, yet its contribution to COVID-19 severity and sex-biased disease remains unclear.
设计与对象
We performed epitranscriptomic profiling on 126 blood samples from non-COVID controls and COVID-19 patients spanning different disease severities. The study included patients with varying levels of disease severity, including ICU patients, and compared them with non-ICU patients.
核心方法
我们在与临床结果相关的 514 个基因中检测到了 1,466 个差异 RNA 编辑位点。该研究涉及对血液样本进行表观转录组分析,以确定与疾病严重程度和性别差异相关的 RNA 编辑模式。
主要结果
Although ADAR expression increased in COVID-19 patients overall, it was strongly reduced in ICU patients compared with non-ICU patients, and this shift was far more prominent in males. This severity-associated and sex-biased ADAR/ADARB1 downregulation coincided with decreased global RNA editing and perturbed expression of immune-related edited transcripts.
解释与创新
组织蛋白酶 S (CTSS) 被确定为中央调节节点:ICU 患者中其 3' UTR 内 A-to I 编辑的增加可稳定 CTSS mRNA。这表明 A 到 I 编辑的破坏是严重程度依赖性的,而不是感染固有的,在危重男性中不成比例地放大。
科研与公卫意义
CTSS 3’UTR editing may serve as a biomarker for patient stratification, revealing a mechanistic connection between epitranscriptomic changes and male-biased severe COVID-19.
局限与证据强度
如果不按疾病严重程度进行分层,ADAR 表达和全局编辑没有显示出显着的性别差异;这种巨大的性别差异仅出现在 ICU 患者中。证据是中等的,因为它基于仅抽象的数据,并且不提供详细的统计或方法学验证。

Severity-dependent and sex-biased dysregulation of A-to-I RNA editing in COVID-19 reveals CTSS as a key regulatory target

Authors: Jing-Yuan Wang, Zhao Ya-di, Li-Ping Wang, Yu-Qing Ren, Jia-Nuo Li, Teng Wang, Hua-Kang Zhang, Feng Zhu, Jian‐Huan Chen, Yun-Yun Jin
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT SARS-CoV-2 infection causes diverse clinical outcomes, with men facing higher risks of severe disease. Adenosine-to-inosine (A-to-I) RNA editing mediated by ADAR enzymes governs innate antiviral immunity, yet its contribution to COVID-19 severity and sex-biased disease remains unclear. We performed epitranscriptomic profiling on 126 blood samples from non-COVID controls and COVID-19 patients spanning different disease severities. We detected 1,466 differentially RNA-edited sites in 514 genes linked to clinical outcomes. Although ADAR expression increased in COVID-19 patients overall, it was strongly reduced in ICU patients compared with non-ICU patients, and this shift was far more prominent in males. This severity-associated and sex-biased ADAR/ADARB1 downregulation coincided with decreased global RNA editing and perturbed expression of immune-related edited transcripts. Cathepsin S (CTSS) was identified as a central regulatory node: elevated A-to-I editing within its 3’ UTR in ICU patients stabilizes CTSS mRNA. Without stratifying by disease severity, ADAR expression and global editing showed no significant sex differences; the large sex divergence only appeared among ICU patients. This indicates severity-dependent, not infection-innate, disruption of A-to-I editing, disproportionately amplified in critically ill males. CTSS 3’UTR editing may serve as a biomarker for patient stratification, revealing a mechanistic connection between epitranscriptomic changes and male-biased severe COVID-19.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
SARS-CoV-2 infection causes diverse clinical outcomes, with men facing higher risks of severe disease. Adenosine-to-inosine (A-to-I) RNA editing mediated by ADAR enzymes governs innate antiviral immunity, yet its contribution to COVID-19 severity and sex-biased disease remains unclear.
Design and population
We performed epitranscriptomic profiling on 126 blood samples from non-COVID controls and COVID-19 patients spanning different disease severities. The study included patients with varying levels of disease severity, including ICU patients, and compared them with non-ICU patients.
Core methods
We detected 1,466 differentially RNA-edited sites in 514 genes linked to clinical outcomes. The study involved epitranscriptomic profiling of blood samples to identify RNA editing patterns associated with disease severity and sex differences.
Main results
Although ADAR expression increased in COVID-19 patients overall, it was strongly reduced in ICU patients compared with non-ICU patients, and this shift was far more prominent in males. This severity-associated and sex-biased ADAR/ADARB1 downregulation coincided with decreased global RNA editing and perturbed expression of immune-related edited transcripts.
Interpretation and novelty
Cathepsin S (CTSS) was identified as a central regulatory node: elevated A-to-I editing within its 3’ UTR in ICU patients stabilizes CTSS mRNA. This indicates severity-dependent, not infection-innate, disruption of A-to-I editing, disproportionately amplified in critically ill males.
Scientific and public-health significance
CTSS 3’UTR editing may serve as a biomarker for patient stratification, revealing a mechanistic connection between epitranscriptomic changes and male-biased severe COVID-19.
Limitations and evidence strength
Without stratifying by disease severity, ADAR expression and global editing showed no significant sex differences; the large sex divergence only appeared among ICU patients. The evidence is moderate as it is based on abstract-only data and does not provide detailed statistical or methodological validation.

Efficacy and Safety of Live Attenuated COVID-19 Vaccine Candidate Based on Omicron-like Temperature-Sensitive Mutant in Preclinical Study

Efficacy and Safety of Live Attenuated COVID-19 Vaccine Candidate Based on Omicron-like Temperature-Sensitive Mutant in Preclinical Study
作者： Е. Б. Файзулоев, Anastasiia V. Gracheva, E. R. Korchevaya, Daria Khokhlova, Asya Maslennikova, Daria Smirnova, Alina Stog, Daria Nagibina, Galina Trunova, Хохлова Варвара Андреевна
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Although the COVID-19 pandemic has ended, emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants remain a future threat.
设计与对象
This study evaluated the safety and efficacy of an intranasal live attenuated vaccine (LAV) prototype based on a temperature-sensitive (ts) Omicron-like SARS-CoV-2 mutant in a Syrian hamster COVID-19 model.
核心方法
通过电子显微镜、纳米孔测序、细胞病变效应滴定和 RT-PCR 对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Dubrovka(武汉样)、Otradnoe(BA.1.1)和 FEB2(BA.5.2)菌株以及源自 FEB2 的 ts F-F3 突变体进行了表征。体外研究使用 Vero CCL-81 细胞;在雌性叙利亚仓鼠中评估了体内传播性和功效研究。通过ELISA和中和测定来测量血清抗体滴度。通过体重变化评估免疫效果;肺部、鼻道和大脑中的病毒载量;攻击后第 4 天进行肺部组织学检查。
主要结果
F-F3菌株在体外表现出稳定的ts表型,不感染仓鼠肺或脑,并且未表现出来自免疫至首次共养动物的传播。F-F3 的单次鼻内免疫诱导了血清转化,在两个独立实验中将肺炎严重程度降低了 76.2% 和 79.5% (p < 0.01),保护肺部免受生产性感染,同时减少了鼻道中的病毒载量,并且在同源 BA.5.2 攻击后没有检测到病毒 传播。在经历类似武汉的异源攻击后,免疫接种阻止了体重减轻,并显着减少了肺部、鼻道和大脑中的病毒载量;然而,对肺炎的保护作用不大(18.6%;p < 0.05)。
解释与创新
Preclinical evaluation of the F-F3-based LAV prototype demonstrated a favorable balance between safety and efficacy. These characteristics support its further consideration as a candidate strain for COVID-19 LAV development.
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,基于 F-F3 的 LAV 可能是在新兴 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体背景下进一步开发的有希望的候选者,特别是其安全性和针对同源挑战的功效。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于叙利亚仓鼠模型的临床前评估,该模型可能无法完全代表人类 免疫应答。证据是温和的,因为它是基于体外和体内研究而没有进行人体试验。

Efficacy and Safety of Live Attenuated COVID-19 Vaccine Candidate Based on Omicron-like Temperature-Sensitive Mutant in Preclinical Study

Authors: Е. Б. Файзулоев, Anastasiia V. Gracheva, E. R. Korchevaya, Daria Khokhlova, Asya Maslennikova, Daria Smirnova, Alina Stog, Daria Nagibina, Galina Trunova, Хохлова Варвара Андреевна
Original abstract (English)
Background/Objectives: Although the COVID-19 pandemic has ended, emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants remain a future threat. This study evaluated the safety and efficacy of an intranasal live attenuated vaccine (LAV) prototype based on a temperature-sensitive (ts) Omicron-like SARS-CoV-2 mutant in a Syrian hamster COVID-19 model. Methods: SARS-CoV-2 Dubrovka (Wuhan-like), Otradnoe (BA.1.1) and FEB2 (BA.5.2) strains and the ts F-F3 mutant derived from FEB2 were characterized by electron microscopy, nanopore sequencing, cytopathic effect titration, and RT-PCR. In vitro studies used Vero CCL-81 cells; in vivo transmissibility and efficacy studies were assessed in female Syrian hamsters. Serum antibody titers were measured by ELISA and neutralization assays. Immunization efficacy was assessed by weight change; viral loads in the lungs, nasal passages, and brain; and histological examination of the lungs at day 4 post-challenge. Safety criteria included evaluation of pathogenicity and transmissibility in hamsters and ts phenotype stability during prolonged in vitro cultivation. Results: The F-F3 strain displayed a stable ts phenotype in vitro, did not infect hamster lungs or brain, and showed no transmission from immunized to naïve co-housed animals. A single intranasal immunization by F-F3 induced seroconversion, reduced pneumonia severity by 76.2% and 79.5% in two independent experiments (p < 0.01), protected the lungs against productive infection while reducing viral load in the nasal passages, and resulted in no detectable viral transmission after homologous BA.5.2 challenge. Following heterologous Wuhan-like challenge, immunization prevented weight loss and significantly reduced viral loads in the lungs, nasal passages, and brain; however, protection against pneumonia was only modest (18.6%; p < 0.05). Conclusions: Preclinical evaluation of the F-F3-based LAV prototype demonstrated a favorable balance between safety and efficacy. These characteristics support its further consideration as a candidate strain for COVID-19 LAV development.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Although the COVID-19 pandemic has ended, emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants remain a future threat.
Design and population
This study evaluated the safety and efficacy of an intranasal live attenuated vaccine (LAV) prototype based on a temperature-sensitive (ts) Omicron-like SARS-CoV-2 mutant in a Syrian hamster COVID-19 model.
Core methods
SARS-CoV-2 Dubrovka (Wuhan-like), Otradnoe (BA.1.1) and FEB2 (BA.5.2) strains and the ts F-F3 mutant derived from FEB2 were characterized by electron microscopy, nanopore sequencing, cytopathic effect titration, and RT-PCR. In vitro studies used Vero CCL-81 cells; in vivo transmissibility and efficacy studies were assessed in female Syrian hamsters. Serum antibody titers were measured by ELISA and neutralization assays. Immunization efficacy was assessed by weight change; viral loads in the lungs, nasal passages, and brain; and histological examination of the lungs at day 4 post-challenge.
Main results
The F-F3 strain displayed a stable ts phenotype in vitro, did not infect hamster lungs or brain, and showed no transmission from immunized to naïve co-housed animals. A single intranasal immunization by F-F3 induced seroconversion, reduced pneumonia severity by 76.2% and 79.5% in two independent experiments (p < 0.01), protected the lungs against productive infection while reducing viral load in the nasal passages, and resulted in no detectable viral transmission after homologous BA.5.2 challenge. Following heterologous Wuhan-like challenge, immunization prevented weight loss and significantly reduced viral loads in the lungs, nasal passages, and brain; however, protection against pneumonia was only modest (18.6%; p < 0.05).
Interpretation and novelty
Preclinical evaluation of the F-F3-based LAV prototype demonstrated a favorable balance between safety and efficacy. These characteristics support its further consideration as a candidate strain for COVID-19 LAV development.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that the F-F3-based LAV could be a promising candidate for further development in the context of emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants, particularly for its safety profile and efficacy against homologous challenges.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to preclinical evaluation in a Syrian hamster model, which may not fully represent human immune responses. The evidence is moderate as it is based on in vitro and in vivo studies without human trials.

Association of In-Hospital Antimalarial–Macrolide Therapy With 3–6-Month Cardiac Electrophysiological Outcomes in Survivors of Severe COVID-19: A Matched Retrospective Cohort Study

Association of In-Hospital Antimalarial–Macrolide Therapy With 3–6-Month Cardiac Electrophysiological Outcomes in Survivors of Severe COVID-19: A Matched Retrospective Cohort Study
作者： Shubhang Sharma Sharma, Julia Ramírez-González, Chávez-Fernández R, Jaime Galindo-Uribe, Jaime Rivera-Figueroa, Payro-Ramírez G, Alejandra Hernández, Fátima Ramos-Pérez, Berenice Hernández-Franco, Diana Torres-Partida
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
This study aimed to assess the cardiac electrophysiological outcomes of survivors of severe COVID-19 pneumonia 3–6 months after hospitalization and compare these outcomes based on exposure to combined antimalarial–macrolide therapy. The background highlights concerns about the potential cardiac effects of these drugs, particularly their ability to prolong ventricular repolarization and increase arrhythmia risk.
设计与对象
A retrospective matched cohort study was conducted involving adults hospitalized for severe COVID-19 pneumonia between March and June 2020. Patients exposed to hydroxychloroquine or chloroquine in combination with azithromycin or clarithromycin were matched 1:1 with patients who did not receive either drug class based on sex and age (±3 years).
核心方法
住院后 3-6 个月的心血管评估包括 12 导联心电图和 24 小时动态心电图监测。该研究评估了 QTc 间期、传导异常、心律失常和心率变异性 (HRV)。
主要结果
包括 86 名患者(43 名暴露者和 43 名未暴露者)。各组之间的随访 QTc 间期相似(420.0 vs. 423.1 ms;p = 0.762),并且 QTc 延长并不常见。未发现恶性室性心律失常或有临床意义的传导异常。暴露患者的窦性心动过缓 患病率 较高,心电图静息心率较低,并且通过正常间期 (SDNN) 标准差评估的 HRV 较高。
解释与创新
The authors concluded that short-term in-hospital exposure to combined antimalarial–macrolide therapy was not associated with persistent QTc prolongation or clinically significant electrophysiological abnormalities at 3–6 months. This study provides novel insights into post-recovery cardiac outcomes in severe COVID-19 survivors.
科研与公卫意义
The findings suggest that combined antimalarial–macrolide therapy during hospitalization for severe COVID-19 may not have long-term cardiac electrophysiological consequences, which could inform future treatment strategies and risk assessments for patients recovering from severe infection.
局限与证据强度
该研究指出,由于急性疾病严重程度可能存在残余混杂因素,因此应谨慎解释心率和 HRV 的差异。证据是中等的,因为该研究是回顾性的并且依赖于观察数据。

Association of In-Hospital Antimalarial–Macrolide Therapy With 3–6-Month Cardiac Electrophysiological Outcomes in Survivors of Severe COVID-19: A Matched Retrospective Cohort Study

Authors: Shubhang Sharma Sharma, Julia Ramírez-González, Chávez-Fernández R, Jaime Galindo-Uribe, Jaime Rivera-Figueroa, Payro-Ramírez G, Alejandra Hernández, Fátima Ramos-Pérez, Berenice Hernández-Franco, Diana Torres-Partida
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background: During the early phase of the COVID-19 pandemic, antimalarial agents and macrolide antibiotics were widely used because of their proposed antiviral and immunomodulatory effects. However, their potential to prolong ventricular repolarization and increase the risk of arrhythmias raised concerns about their cardiac electrophysiological effects. Available evidence has predominantly focused on electrophysiological effects during acute treatment, whereas post-recovery outcomes remain less well characterized. Objectives: To evaluate cardiac electrophysiological outcomes 3–6 months after hospitalization for severe COVID-19 pneumonia and compare these outcomes according to in-hospital exposure to combined antimalarial–macrolide therapy. Methods: We conducted a retrospective matched cohort study including adults hospitalized for severe COVID-19 pneumonia between March and June 2020. Patients exposed to hydroxychloroquine or chloroquine in combination with azithromycin or clarithromycin were matched 1:1 by sex and age (±3 years) with patients who did not receive either drug class. Cardiovascular assessment 3–6 months after hospitalization included a 12-lead electrocardiogram and 24-hour Holter monitoring. QTc interval, conduction abnormalities, arrhythmias, and heart rate variability (HRV) were evaluated. Results: Eighty-six patients were included (43 exposed and 43 unexposed). Follow-up QTc intervals were similar between groups (420.0 vs. 423.1 ms; p =0.762), and QTc prolongation was uncommon. No malignant ventricular arrhythmias or clinically significant conduction abnormalities were identified. Exposed patients had a higher prevalence of sinus bradycardia, a lower resting heart rate on electrocardiography, and higher HRV as assessed by the standard deviation of normal-to-normal intervals (SDNN). However, unexposed patients exhibited a greater inflammatory burden during hospitalization, more frequent intensive care unit admissions, and longer hospital stays. Conclusion: In this cohort of survivors of severe COVID-19, short-term in-hospital exposure to combined antimalarial–macrolide therapy was not associated with persistent QTc prolongation or clinically significant electrophysiological abnormalities at 3–6 months. Differences in heart rate and HRV should be interpreted with caution, given potential residual confounding due to acute disease severity.
View research seven-element analysis
